## 1. What this report holds and how to read it

A collection: the member words, the Strong's codes behind them, and what the study has recorded for the occurrences of those words, from the base data through the observations and the claims into the narrative. Sections 2 and 3 say what the data is; 4 and 5 say what the study has recorded; 6 lists the phrases; 7 lists the faults found in the stored rows. Verses are in Scripture order (book in cfg_book_order, chapter, verse). The detail that does not fit a reading (every Strong's entry in full, every observation and node, the change history, the narrative text) is in the workbook, one sheet for each listing; the sheet is named where it is cited.

## 4. What the study has recorded for these occurrences

Of 992 occurrences, 554 have an engagement observation and 438 have none recorded. There are 301 live observations on them (16 engagement, 240 of other recorded stages, 45 of the retired catalogue stages) and 17 withdrawn.

### 4.1 Engagement: what each word does for the inner being (16 observations)

An engagement observation states, for the surface in a verse, whether it voices an inner-being operation (use, face) or is silent. Each is one statement applied to every occurrence it covers.

- **Observation 21469** (engagement, tag face, status draft): Righteous is here the godliness, devoutness, reverence or uprightness of a person as a state of the heart toward God or toward what is right (a devout man who feared God, godliness trained and pursued, uprightness of heart, reverence and awe).
  - applies to 55 occurrence(s): Gen 6:9 (righteous), Gen 7:1 (righteous), Gen 38:26 (righteous), 1Sa 24:17 (righteous), 2Sa 4:11 (righteous), 1Ki 2:32 (righteous), Job 15:14 (righteous), Job 17:9 (righteous), Job 32:1 (righteous), Job 35:7 (righteous), Psa 37:21 (righteous), Psa 37:30 (righteous); and 43 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 21477** (engagement, tag face, status draft): Righteousness is here the godliness, devoutness, reverence or uprightness of a person as a state of the heart toward God or toward what is right (a devout man who feared God, godliness trained and pursued, uprightness of heart, reverence and awe).
  - applies to 131 occurrence(s): Gen 18:19 (righteousness), Lev 19:15 (righteousness), Deu 9:4 (righteousness), Deu 9:5 (righteousness), Deu 9:6 (righteousness), 1Sa 26:23 (righteousness), 2Sa 22:21 (righteousness), 2Sa 22:25 (righteousness), 1Ki 3:6 (righteousness), 1Ki 8:32 (righteousness), 1Ki 10:9 (righteousness), 2Ch 6:23 (righteousness); and 119 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 21511** (engagement, tag face, status draft): Righteousness is here the righteous life God requires of a person, which the anger of man does not produce: the moral state of the person.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 1:20 (righteousness)
- **Observation 21471** (engagement, tag qualification, status draft): Righteous gives a moral condition to what it binds (an upright heart, a righteous or wicked way, godly grief, walking uprightly, a sinful nation): the uprightness, godliness or wickedness of the heart, speech, way or conduct.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Deu 16:18 (righteous), Pro 8:8 (righteous), Pro 16:13 (righteous), Pro 16:31 (righteous), Isa 58:2 (righteous), 1Th 2:10 (righteous)

The anger is at a self that has made itself right; Outside the verse:; After, Job 32:8: "it is the spirit in man, the breath of the Almighty, that makes him understand". Genuine. Elihu justifies speaking despite his youth; Further on, Job 32:18: "the spirit within me constrains me". Genuine, the bearer's own. New here (outside the ±3 window; read in this session). The seat of Elihu's released anger arrives as a spirit under pressure, with the belly as its bodily image.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 32:2 (justified)
- **Observation 14279** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Where it comes from. Every verse, or the one next to it, names a source, and the sources are inner: Passions at war within: "What causes quarrels and what causes fights among you? Is it not this, that your passions are at war within you? You desire and do not have, so you murder. You covet and cannot obtain, so you fight and quarrel" (Jam 4:1–2). The outer fight is the inner war carried outward; The flesh: "for you are still of the flesh. - **Observation 15693** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): G4205 (pornos, 10 verses) functions almost entirely as a vice-list category, who is excluded from the kingdom, paired consistently with idolaters as a fixed collocation (1 Cor 5:10-11, 6:9, Eph 5:5, Rev 21:8, 22:15), reinforcing the same adultery and idolatry pairing from a different angle.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Eph 5:5 (sexually immoral), Rev 21:8 (immoral), Rev 22:15 (immoral)
- **Observation 15699** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Avel and avlah (H5766A/B): in Ezekiel's repeated formula, when a righteous person turns away from his righteousness and does injustice he shall die (Ezek 3:20, 18:24, 18:26, 33:13, 33:18). Injustice is a moral reversal from a prior righteous state, relapse and not a starting condition, unlike guilt's recognition-model or cruelty's other-attribution, which describe a condition someone simply has or falls into.
  - applies to 10 occurrence(s): Eze 3:20 (righteous), Eze 3:20 (righteousness), Eze 18:24 (righteous person), Eze 18:24 (righteousness), Eze 18:26 (righteous person), Eze 18:26 (righteousness), Eze 33:13 (righteous), Eze 33:13 (righteousness), Eze 33:18 (righteous), Eze 33:18 (righteousness)
- **Observation 15704** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The justice and judiciary sub-theme recurs strongly: H5557 (salaph) and H5791 (avat) are both explicitly about bribery and perverting legal judgment (a bribe subverts the cause of the righteous, Exod 23:8, Deut 16:19; does God pervert justice, Job 8:3, 34:12), and H6127 and H6140 apply the same crooked language to justice itself (justice goes forth perverted, Hab 1:4; make crooked all that is straight, Mic 3:9). This is the third family, after D and G, to converge independently on judicial and legal-fairness vocabulary: corruption of justice specifically, not wrongdoing in general.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 16:19 (righteous)
- **Observation 15737** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): Pole 2 of the split, deliberate deception exercised as an act on someone else: G4106 (planē) is the richest single entry, ranging from fraud (the guards' report of a stolen body, Matt 27:64) to organized false teaching (Eph 4:14; 2 Thess 2:11's strong delusion; 1 John 4:6's spirit of error set against the Spirit of truth) to a term for entrenched moral corruption (Rom 1:27; Jude 11, Cain, Balaam and Korah as paradigm cases).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Jo 4:6 (truth)
- **Observation 15764** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): porneia used relationally and identity-forming: we were not born of sexual immorality (John 8:41, the Pharisees' claim of legitimate parentage) and she refuses to repent of her sexual immorality (Rev 2:21, a named individual, Jezebel): the noun tied to birth-legitimacy and to one person's persistent unrepentance, not a general category.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Joh 8:41 (sexual immorality), Rev 2:21 (sexual immorality)
- **Observation 15765** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): porneia paired directly with idolatry in the death-list of vices to put to death (Col 3:5, covetousness, which is idolatry): the same equation LSJ notes for the word itself, metaphorically idolatry.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Col 3:5 (immorality)
- **Observation 15773** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): 1 Cor 10:8 carries porneuō twice with two different morphs in one verse: a present active subjunctive (let us not indulge, hortatory, the Corinthians' live present temptation) and an aorist active indicative (as some of them did, the wilderness generation's completed past act): the tense shift alone marks the boundary between the historical warning-example and the present exhortation it supports.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 10:8 (sexual immorality)
- **Observation 15774** (prior-capture, tag term, status draft): Rev 19:2 and Col 3:5 both render porneia as bare immorality rather than sexual immorality: the ESV drops the sexual qualifier at exactly the two points where the word is used most abstractly or metaphorically (Babylon's corrupting influence on the earth; a general vice-list entry next to impurity, passion, evil desire), while every occurrence naming a concrete act or a named person keeps sexual. The English surface itself tracks the literal and metaphorical distinction, even though the underlying Greek word does not formally mark it.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Col 3:5 (immorality), Rev 19:2 (immorality)

## 7. Stored-data faults

Blatant errors and omissions in the stored rows, each found by the rule stated. A fault is shown with the rows that identify it; the first 25 of a long list are shown and every row is in the workbook sheet `Faults`.

| rule | what it looks for | found |
|---|---|---|
| link-to-missing-or-deleted-term | live collection_link of this collection whose term_id has no live surface_term row | 0 |
| collection-merge-inconsistent | collection.status and collection.merged_into_id disagree, or the target is not live | 0 |
| member-surface-with-no-occurrence | member term of origin 'surface' that no live verse_lexical row carries | 0 |
| occurrence-in-deleted-verse | occurrence whose verse row is soft deleted | 0 |
| live-node-on-deleted-observation | ib_node with deleted=0 whose observation is deleted or missing (the node still names a verse and word the observation no longer covers) | 0 |
| claim-source-on-deleted-observation | narrative_claim_source naming an observation that is deleted or missing | 0 |
| claim-in-narrative-with-no-current-telling | narrative_claim.in_narrative=1 and no current narrative_claim_telling | 0 |
| live-node-with-no-verse-lexical-id | live ib_node in a verse of the report with a member surface but verse_lexical_id NULL (stored, tied to no occurrence) | 0 |

## 8. Sources

Occurrences: live verse_lexical rows, with the verse text from table `verse`; Strong's data from `strong`, `strong_sense`, `strong_meaning_tree`, `strong_related`, `cluster_strong`; observations and nodes from `ib_observation` and `ib_node` (an observation is withdrawn when deleted = 1); claims from `narrative_claim_source` (by observation_id or verse_id), `narrative_claim`, `narrative_claim_telling` and `narrative_section`. A table or column was used only after its recorded meaning in `cfg_table` and `cfg_column` was read (rule B:123).
